0e887332c1f9965dca80ede405c051048b4f75ae 2026-09-02 10:23:31 -0300 Rafael
    fix(demo): pick the venv interpreter by version, and guard the release asset in CI
`make demo` is the README's headline command and it was broken on the most common
machine there is. `PYTHON ?= $(shell command -v python3.11 || command -v python3)`
selects an interpreter by NAME, but pyproject's requires-python is `>=3.10,<3.12`.
On any box without a `python3.11` binary — where `python3` is 3.12+, the current
macOS/Homebrew default and the default on this repo's own dev machine — the recipe
built `.venv` from it and pip then refused:

    ERROR: Package 'personacore' requires a different Python: 3.12.13 not in '<3.12,>=3.10'

reproduced verbatim. The failure is worse than a bad first run: `.venv` survives it
and satisfies its own file prerequisite, so every later `make demo` fails the same
way, and nothing in the message ever names Python 3.11 as the fix.

`PY_SUPPORTED` is now the single spelling of the bound and each candidate is asked
its version rather than trusted for its name — a `python3` that happens to BE 3.11
still works, a 3.12+ one is skipped, and when none qualifies the recipe stops before
creating anything with an install hint. The `demo` target re-runs the same check
against an already-present `.venv`, because the file prerequisite cannot see inside
a directory an earlier run (or an earlier Makefile) left behind.

Verified on the four paths: 3.11 selected when present; no supported interpreter ->
fail fast, no `.venv` created; a bare `python3` that is 3.11 -> still selected; a
pre-existing 3.12 `.venv` -> refused before pip with the removal instruction.

CI could not have caught any of this, and it also could not catch the pin going
stale. The suite injects a fake `fetch` — correctly, so no job pulls 55.6 MB — which
leaves NOTHING able to tell whether the public m1-demo-v1 asset is still downloadable
or still hashes to `DEFAULT_SHA256`; `test_default_pin_is_the_release_digest` compares
the pin against a second hand-written copy of the same digest, so two spellings of a
stale value agree perfectly. The new `demo-asset` job runs the real helper against the
real release over the same anonymous URL a stranger uses (verified end to end here:
55,601,269 bytes, digest matched, ~44s), on a weekly cron as well as on push, because
link rot does not wait for a push.

The three new tests are anchored to the parsed assignment lines, not grepped from the
file — the comment block above them names `python3` and `3.12` on purpose, so a
file-wide search would measure the prose. All three go naturally RED against the
pre-fix Makefile and green after.

    fix(25-14): the watcher crash-looped in the window before the sweep's first beat
[Rule 1] `read_last_beat` called `Path(...).read_text()` unguarded, so an absent
heartbeat raised FileNotFoundError. Measured while building the two LaunchAgents:
the watcher plist carries `StartInterval 60` and the operator bootstraps it BEFORE
kickstarting the sweep, while `data/phase25_heartbeat.jsonl` is not created until
the driver's first beat. For that whole window the watcher exited non-zero once a
minute and wrote a traceback into logs/phase25_watch.err — in exactly the state the
module's own docstring calls normal ('an empty heartbeat is the normal state between
the LaunchAgent starting and the driver's first beat, so it is not a stall').

Worse than noise: a LaunchAgent-managed job that exits non-zero on a schedule is the
shape that invites automatic remediation, which is the whole thing D-16 forbids —
and the same test file already pins the inverse property (a DETECTED stall exits 0
so the watcher never looks like a crashing job).

Fixed at the shared root rather than at the caller: `check()` is the only caller
today, but the guard belongs where the read is. Two lines, and `exists` is not in
FORBIDDEN_ACTIONS so the AST never-act walk is untouched.

tests/test_phase25_watch.py::test_an_absent_heartbeat_is_not_a_stall_either asserts
the same three outcomes as the existing empty-file test plus a real main() exit 0.

    fix(24-08): refuse a degenerate refusal template at the boundary (24-REVIEW CR-03)
`contains_refusal` was `any(normalize(t) in normalize(completion) ...)`.
`normalize` strips edge punctuation and collapses whitespace, so "", "   " and
"..." all normalize to "" — and "" is a substring of everything. Measured:
contains_refusal("the answer is blue", [""]) -> True for every completion, and
score_refusal(["a","b","c"], [""]) -> (3, 3).

D-04 exists to separate "refused under attack" from "answered wrong" from
"refuses everywhere", and 100% on both populations IS the refuses-everywhere
verdict — the most alarming reading the instrument can produce, manufactured
from an input error with no signal. Under D-07 the same column is the n=64
memorization sensor.

- one `_prove` guard in `contains_refusal`, the module's own refusal idiom.
  NOT in `score_refusal` and not in a new shared validator: the wrapper routes
  every template through this predicate, so one guard covers both entry points
  and there is no second copy to drift
- named residue: `score_refusal([], [""])` still returns (0, 0) — zero
  completions never reach the predicate, and no reading is produced there

re-litigated at execution: the refusal table and the episode builder share one
module `scripts/phase24_adversarial.py`, not frozen (D-05 must be able to move

instrument and the table stay separately owned; the two D-13 assertions live in one
module as two separately-named tests.

Two things the record could not previously support:

- RAW PER-ITEM EVIDENCE. `evidence[].per_question` is one row per QUESTION — the unit

  gitignored ledger. 864 rows/seed, ~124 KB.

  in a different process, through the same imported `phase18_extraction.score_records`,
  and the result asserted EQUAL to the recorded count. A disagreement means the block and
  its own evidence describe different runs, and the write aborts.

Done at assembly rather than at scoring time so all five seeds get identical treatment
whenever they were scored, and so the guard compares two independent passes instead of
copying one field. Verified on the already-scored seed 1337: re-derives 0/416.

The raw completion TEXT stays in gitignored `data/` with its sha256 pinned in the
record — it is ~1 MB of model output per seed, not a measurement.

  they are produced, and a re-launch REUSES them. A kill now costs at most one SHAPE
  (~30 min) instead of one seed (~2.3 h), and a post-processing defect costs seconds.
  The cache is REFUSED unless its adapter digest, corpus digest and k all match.
- The block is json.dumps-proved inside `score_never_taught`, where the failure is free,
  instead of at `_state_write` after the generation is spent.

  tautologically true there. It is a reader-visible marker, not a live refusal.

  demanding". On the one state that matters it is strictly more PERMISSIVE —
  that is why it exists. What is stronger is its SHAPE: seven NAMED conjuncts

a629d93d5b42e899aedb723101554a758d9fc2fd 2026-08-27 18:22:57 -0300 Rafael
    revert(23-20): discard seed 2025's partial training bytes — no reading, no adapter
Seed 2025's 5-row `run.csv` (steps 10-50) and its step-~50 `latest.pt` are
removed so `train_matched_control(2025)` can retrain from scratch. This is NOT
a laundering of the one-attempt rule, in four parts:

1. SEED 2025 PRODUCED NO READING. It has no `primary` block, no entry in
   `data/phase23_run_state.json`'s `matched` section at all, and no adapter.
   `matched()`'s own comment (scripts/phase23_run.py:2156-2158) states the
   governing distinction verbatim: "THE PREDICATE IS 'SCORED', NOT 'TRAINED'
   ... a training leg killed mid-run and resumed is LEGITIMATE and must stay
   legitimate. Only a seed that already produced a READING is evidence of a
   completed prior attempt." What is deleted is evidence of WHERE THE KILL
   LANDED, not a measurement.

2. A MID-TRAIN RESUME IS REFUSED BY A COMMITTED MECHANISM, so discarding is the
   only admissible completion. `prove_matched_protocol` refuses `resume_from`
   in the comparator's kwargs (scripts/phase23_run.py:1181-1187), and
   `phase23_matched_prereg.DP_FN_BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS` items 6 and 7 are
   dispositioned `unreached` PRECISELY because this scheduling resumes nothing.
   Passing `resume_from` would reach two branches the BLIND ledger declared
   unreachable — a protocol change, in the one place this phase cannot make
   one. D-07's `refuse_if_exists(expected=...)` inversion exists and is
   deliberately NOT used here for that reason. Retraining under
   `seed_everything(2025)` on the same pinned corpus is what the pin declares.

3. THE DELETION IS A VISIBLE DIFF, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. `run.csv` is
   tracked as of `d99d2aa`, so this removal appears in `git log
   --diff-filter=D`. Recovery is:

       git show d99d2aa:results/phase23_matched_control_seed2025/run.csv

   BOTH HALVES: the `latest.pt` is gitignored (`.gitignore:14`) and is NOT
   recoverable from git. It is deterministically regenerable from
   `seed_everything(2025)` plus the pinned corpus, and this commit plus
   23-17-SUMMARY.md:162 are its record. A recovery claim that omitted the
   unrecoverable half would be the overclaim this phase exists to refuse.

4. NOTHING SCORED IS TOUCHED. Verified, not asserted:
   `git diff --exit-code d99d2aa HEAD -- results/phase23_matched_control_seed1337
   results/phase23_matched_control_seed2024 results/phase23_matched_control_seed1338
   data/phase23_run_state.json` exits 0.

    test(21-06): N1-N6 mutate-between-calls — the fact map is consumed at RUN TIME (D-06)
The instrument that separates run time from build time: mutate the fact bin BETWEEN two
calls in one process, with the other two bins proven byte-frozen by sha256.

- N1 deleted        -> ValueError "could not be opened", names the *_fact.bin path
- N2 truncated by 1 -> "not 1:1", names all three paths and 8449/8449/8448
- N3 rolled by 1    -> INPUT-space IMPURE; length, id multiset and block remainder all
                       asserted UNCHANGED first, and target space asserted [] on the roll
- N4 interior flip  -> IMPURE on EXACTLY one window ("window 0 carries ids [0, 1]", one
                       "carries ids" occurrence), then a sha256-proven restore returns
                       call 2 to call 1's exact tensors
- N5 unmutated      -> negative control; without it a loader that ALWAYS raised passes N1-N4
- N6 token mutated  -> fact_index unchanged while x differs — proves WHICH file is read

Plus: test_valid_bin_never_raises_on_any_fact (a target-space draw check would redden on
every fact but the last, on a correct bin — this makes that a RED, not a mystery) and
test_grad_accum_steps_equals_n_facts, which reads the count off the loop's actual returns
and asserts one lot covers all 33 windows exactly once.

Observed on the real bin: fact_index sequence [0..7], wrap at step=8 -> 0, batch shapes
(4,4,4,4,4,5,4,4) x 256 = D-01's windows_per_fact, target-space rows [3,7,11,15,19,24,28]
= n_facts - 1 = 7.

The RED fired at N=64 ONLY: 1e-3 * 8 = 0.008 still clears the 0.01 ceiling, so the
n=8 guard PASSED under the same mutation. The n=64 row is therefore load-bearing
rather than a stronger-sounding duplicate — measured, not argued.

DEVIATION from the plan's literal <behavior> ("equals 0.8122523963562354"): the
rejected-recipe products are asserted with pytest.approx(rel=1e-12), not ==.
n ** -1.1 is a non-integer power routed through libm pow, which is not bit-identical
across CPU families; commit 4554c93 in this repo is a fix for exactly that class
(arm64 Darwin vs x86_64 Linux CI). 1e-12 is ~4 orders looser than a 1-ulp libm
disagreement and ~14 orders tighter than the 66x-81x effect asserted, so it cannot
mask the finding. The >= 0.01 inequalities are left exact — 66x of margin needs no slack.

 3 files changed, 477 insertions(+), 23 deletions(-)
e144417face737ec3f67c10609f750b19a6ddb79 2026-08-20 11:33:42 -0300 Rafael
    docs: define milestone v4.0 requirements (48 REQ-IDs, 10 categories)
Evaluation cost resolved before pre-registration closes, per the standing
rule that a resource budget may be measured beforehand but an outcome
threshold may not.

Research reported the evaluation leg as unmeasured (STACK's generation probe
failed and it correctly asserted no number). The measurement already existed:
Phase 18's D-12 pre-flight, results/phase18_preflight_report.md, committed
with git_sha/pid/seed/corpus-sha256. Replicating that report's own projection
from its four per-shape rates reproduces its stated 84,960 draws / 9.54 h
exactly, validating the cost model against a committed artifact.

  per sweep point at full Phase-18 fidelity = 42,480 draws = 4.77 h
  training per point                        = ~17 s
  ratio                                     = ~1,010x

Evaluation is the binding constraint by three orders of magnitude. A 16-point
sweep (2 arms x 2 capacities x 4 points) costs 76.3 h at K=48. CAL-04 therefore
requires per-point K and the promotion-to-full-fidelity rule to be committed
before any v4.0 artifact exists — phase18_extraction.py:88-92 records that
reducing K after seeing a null is the weakening ATK-03/P18-4 exist to prevent,
and that pre-flight is the one moment the pin leaves open for it. v4.0 is at
that same moment.

CAL-05 records the honest bound: the rate was measured on the un-adapted base
where 45-56/64 draws stop-terminated. A noised adapter that stops emitting EOS
runs max_new_tokens=48 every draw, so 4.77 h/point is a floor for noised
points, not a mean.

Also recorded as new debt (not fixed): phase18_extraction.py:85-87 cites four
throughput rates its own cited artifact does not contain (146.98/127.70/
178.74/130.31 vs the report's 145.01/134.54/183.20/140.85; 9.91 h vs 9.54 h).
No published result changes — K=48 holds under either set. The file is
ancestry-guarded and v3.0's lesson is that correction prose is denser in
exactly the facts that are easy to get wrong.

Synthesizes STACK/FEATURES/ARCHITECTURE/PITFALLS into SUMMARY.md, resolving
five forks between the four researchers rather than averaging them:

  side gave. Amplification at q=0.103 is worth 12.9x at fixed T (STACK's
  'nearly gone' is false), and is cancelled by the group-privacy conversion to
  a fact-level claim, which drives delta above 1 at k>=10.

  so module hooks do not reach them. Ghost-clipping rejection survives on the
  math structure; FEATURES' hook mechanism and ~1.2x both fall; ARCHITECTURE's
  vmap anti-pattern falls on measurement.

  the real constraint is corpus size.
- Gate condition (c) specified concretely against committed erasure_gate
  constants and the measured dialogue PPL noise floor.

29a86e5aebf63538c284efbc60ff4b803dcba571 2026-08-20 09:12:51 -0300 Rafael
    docs: correct v4.0 scope from research — three-condition gate, measured DP cost, privacy unit
Four parallel researchers returned findings that falsify three claims this
section made when written at 4f0423b. Corrected in place: the v4.0 scope is a
live plan, not a closed pre-registration, and nothing has been gated on it.

1. The gate had two conditions and claimed to answer Phase 19's failure mode.
   It did not. erasure_gate.py carried three and says in its own text that its
   third exists because the first two 'can BOTH be satisfied by a model
   degraded into uselessness' — which is what Phase 19 did, destroying 77.6%
   of dialogue adaptation while its target condition cleared. Added condition
   (c) general capability >= C.
2. '~B× for naive batch-1 per-example gradients' was an unverified
   extrapolation from estimate_fisher. Measured on the real model at
   production shape on MPS: naive ~3× (3.31×/2.97×), vmap(grad) 1.07× at B=8.
   Also false: 'the adapter surface is pure nn.Linear' — lora_A/lora_B are
   bare nn.Parameters in an inline matmul (lora/layer.py:41).
3. 'The DP-SGD cost number gates the sweep design' — training is ~17 s per
   arm, so it does not. Evaluation or corpus size binds; calibration must
   measure both legs.

Also added the privacy-unit requirement: a fact is carried by 22 rendered rows
(counted by executing phase14_factset.render_family, 176 rows / 8 LOCKED_FACTS;
corrected up from an inferred 14), so example-level epsilon bounds nothing
about a fact.
